- **g6-abstraction**: Use when each evaluation in a black-box search is costly (a training run, a simulation, a benchmark), you hold a trace of configurations already evaluated with their scores, and you are about to spend more budget on blind trial and error; it fits a checked model of the score landscape to the trace, measures on the recorded trace what pruning would have saved or lost, and gives guarded advice on which fresh candidates cannot beat the incumbent. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/abstraction/skill))
- **g6-action-gating**: Use when an agent proposes an outward action and needs a deterministic decision before acting, when a workflow must stay inside the bounds a person reviewed it for, or when the person wants per-run operation count limits and injected safeguards that can only tighten that decision. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/action_gating/skill))
- **g6-action-logic**: Use when an agent or workflow is about to take a large or hard-to-undo step and must check that it has a complete plan to observe, stop and roll it back, that it does not collide with other actions in flight, that no warning signal is being ignored, or that it is not still relying on a hypothesis an outcome has already disproved. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/action_logic/skill))
- **g6-adapt-audio**: Use when you need to cut, join, mix, fade, level, equalise, compress, limit, gate, de-ess, de-noise, resample or convert a WAV, or measure its loudness, true peak, pitch, tempo, spectrum or silences, and you want every result measured again before you use it, never clipped silently. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_audio/skill))
- **g6-adapt-automl**: Use when you want the best of several scikit-learn estimators and their settings for a table, found by a bounded, seeded search (a time budget and a trial cap) that compares settings by cross-validation on training rows only, with the winner measured on rows the tool held out itself against a baseline, and a missing search engine refused rather than silently replaced. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/ml/adapt_automl/skill))
- **g6-adapt-bayesian**: Use when a scalar beta-binomial, gamma-Poisson or normal mean model needs an explicit posterior, credible interval, prior sensitivity, predictive check or checked sampling, and a point estimate would hide uncertainty. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/ml/adapt_bayesian/skill))
- **g6-adapt-blender**: Use when you need a 3D scene built, lit, rendered or exported (OBJ, STL, GLB, FBX) with the Blender the person installed, and you want every change read back from Blender's scene and every render and export inspected before you use it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_blender/skill))
- **g6-adapt-comfyui**: Use when you need a picture made or edited from a text prompt by a diffusion model on the ComfyUI server the person runs on this machine (text to image, image to image, inpainting, upscaling, or your own ComfyUI workflow), with every picture fetched into G6's media folder and checked before you use it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_comfyui/skill))
- **g6-adapt-diagrams**: Use when you need a diagram (a flowchart, a sequence of messages, a graph, a component map, a box, tree or table in plain text) written as Mermaid, Graphviz DOT, PlantUML or ASCII that will not break on a quote or a keyword, or drawn by Graphviz into an SVG or PNG you can trust holds every node you declared. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_diagrams/skill))
- **g6-adapt-django**: Use when drafting consistent Django model, view, URL, admin, form, serializer and settings source, then checking a complete project in the sandbox. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/code/adapt_django/skill))
- **g6-adapt-ffmpeg**: Use when you need to convert, cut, join, resize, retime, filter, subtitle or level a video or sound file with ffmpeg, or pull out its sound or a frame, and you want every file ffmpeg writes measured against what you asked for before you use it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_ffmpeg/skill))
- **g6-adapt-generative-art**: Use when you need an actual picture file and not a description of one (procedural art, a texture, a pattern, a fractal, a vector drawing or a terrain mesh for a 3D scene), made on this machine with no model, no network and no cost, from a seed you can reproduce, and checked before you use it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_generative_art/skill))
- **g6-adapt-healing**: Use when one step of Python code fails with an error you have tests for, and an agent wants a model to repair it without a careless automatic fix; the error is triaged first, the patch is checked in the sandbox on tests the model sees, and it is kept only if it passes held-back tests the model never saw. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/code/adapt_healing/skill))
- **g6-adapt-image**: Use when you need to resize, crop, rotate, flip, recolour, combine or convert a picture, measure one strictly, or have one generated (locally through ComfyUI or a procedural technique, or by a paid image service only when G6's action gate allows the payment), and you want every resulting file checked before you use it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_image/skill))
- **g6-adapt-library-synthesizer**: Use when translating a paper's algorithm into a Python module and checking it against examples supplied independently by the caller. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/code/adapt_library_synthesizer/skill))
- **g6-adapt-pandas**: Use when you are handed a table (CSV text, JSON rows or a list of rows) and need to know, before you compute anything from it, which columns are numbers, text or a mix, which values only look missing, which codes have leading zeros, and which rows or keys repeat, read by one stated rule with nothing converted or dropped silently. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/ml/adapt_pandas/skill))
- **g6-adapt-physical-ai**: Use when a robot motion plan or grasp, from a model, a planner or a person, is about to be carried out and you need to know whether it collides along its segments, leaves its workspace, breaks a joint or speed limit, misses its goal, or lets an object slip or crush, against the robot's own specification and a stated world. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/embodied/adapt_physical_ai/skill))
- **g6-adapt-pytorch**: Use when you need a small neural network trained on rows of data without writing the training loop yourself, and need its score measured on rows held out by the tool itself, against a baseline, with the run seeded and stopped the moment its loss breaks, so a training accuracy, a diverged loss or a leaky feature never passes for a result. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/ml/adapt_pytorch/skill))
- **g6-adapt-rq**: Use when you need to submit an operator-registered background job, inspect queue reports or recover selected failed jobs, with JSON arguments, operator permission and a separately managed Redis or Valkey server and POSIX worker. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/integrations/adapt_rq/skill))
- **g6-adapt-sklearn**: Use when you need to fit a scikit-learn model to rows of data, or compare a few, and need its score measured on rows held out by the tool itself, against a baseline, with an interval, after checks for target leakage, repeated rows, class imbalance and a fixed seed, so a training score or a leaky feature never passes for a result. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/ml/adapt_sklearn/skill))
- **g6-adapt-synth**: Use when you need a tone, a note, a chord, an arpeggio, a scale, a short melody, noise or a drum pattern as a WAV file, or want a few words read into musical parameters, and you want each sound measured against what you asked for before you use it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_synth/skill))
- **g6-adapt-visualisation**: Use when you need a chart of rows of data (a line, bars, a scatter, a histogram, box plots or a heat map) as an SVG, PNG or self-contained HTML file, and need to know it shows every row you gave and only the values you gave, with nothing dropped silently and no script in the file. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_visualisation/skill))
- **g6-adapt-voice**: Use when you need text spoken into a WAV file (a local voice first, a paid one only when the person's key file and G6's action gate allow it), or a recording transcribed by a paid service, and you want each voice inspected, and a transcript refused when it is empty or too fast for the speech in the clip (its words are not checked), before you use it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/media/adapt_voice/skill))
- **g6-adapt-webpage**: Use when building an offline web page or design-token set and checking markup, declared colour contrast and optional horizontal overflow. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/code/adapt_webpage/skill))
- **g6-agent-autogen**: Use when a task should be worked by a team of AutoGen agents (taking turns, picked by a model, or handing the task over) on a model the person passes in, with a message limit G6's trusted policy caps, every tool call decided by G6's gate, G6's stop checked after every message, and the team's answer kept as a draft to check. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/agents/agent_autogen/skill))
- **g6-agent-claude**: Use when a coding task should be handed to Anthropic's Claude Code, the command-line coding agent the person installed and signed in to, in one project folder, with its own tools named on every run (read tools only unless edits are granted, never its shell or web tools), a deadline, G6's stop, and its own report kept as a claim to check. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/agents/agent_claude/skill))
- **g6-agent-codex**: Use when a coding task should be handed to OpenAI's Codex, the command-line coding agent the person installed and signed in to, in one project folder, with no more access than the task needs (read-only unless writes are granted), a deadline, G6's stop, and Codex's own report kept as a claim to check. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/agents/agent_codex/skill))
- **g6-agent-langgraph**: Use when a multi-step workflow should run as a LangGraph graph that saves its state after every step under G6's state folder, so a run that is stopped, killed or fails resumes where it was without repeating finished steps, with every tool call decided by G6's gate, llm steps only on a model the person passes in, and the result kept as a draft to check. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/agents/agent_langgraph/skill))
- **g6-agent-nanoclaw**: Use when several agents must pass messages to one another durably (groups of named members, messages to one member or to all, optionally typed), with every message reaching its reader at least once, in order, a page at a time, coming back until it is acknowledged, a retried send stored once, and none reaching the wrong member. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/agents/agent_nanoclaw/skill))
- **g6-agent-runtime**: Use when an agent runtime (a coding agent's command line, a framework's agent loop, your own adapter) is to run a task under limits a person set, with every tool call decided before it runs, the run stoppable, every failure handled the same way, and the agent's own "done" kept as a claim rather than taken as proof. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/agents/agent_runtime/skill))
- **g6-agent-smolagents**: Use when a task suits a smolagents agent, either calling declared tools or writing Python to work it out, on a model the person passes in, with every tool call decided by G6's gate and every code step held to a restricted syntax and run in a bounded child process (a clean environment, a time limit, no network, a fixed import list), never in G6's process, and the answer kept as a draft to check. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/agents/agent_smolagents/skill))
- **g6-algebra**: Use when an answer, a test expectation or code rests on a symbolic calculation (simplifying, factoring, solving an equation, a derivative, an integral, a limit, a series, a matrix's determinant, inverse or eigenvalues, or an integer's factors, primality or greatest common divisor) and you are about to state the result from your own working. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/algebra/skill))
- **g6-algorithm-design**: Use when a task needs an algorithm and you want catalogued textbook options that fit the stated problem, each with the preconditions it needs, the cost it claims and the proof obligations it leaves, bound to the requirements a change must keep, instead of a method invented on the spot or one called optimal without proof. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/algorithm_design/skill))
- **g6-align-prompt-library**: Use when prompts, specifications or evaluation sets must be kept with their full history, each version addressed by the SHA-256 of its content so a result can cite exactly the version it used, with strict template rendering, tombstones instead of deletes and rollback as a new version. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/workspace/align_prompt_library/skill))
- **g6-australian-legal-corpus**: Use when an agent must find what Australian legislation or a court decision says, in the text itself with its citation, jurisdiction, link and collection date, rather than from memory; it searches a local copy of the Open Australian Legal Corpus, refuses Victoria, the ACT, the Northern Territory and any jurisdiction it lacks instead of answering with another's law, and says every time that the text is a dated snapshot, not current law and not legal advice. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/sources/australian_legal_corpus/skill))
- **g6-causal**: Use when an answer says one thing causes, drives or improves another, or estimates an effect from data, and you are about to choose which variables to control for, call an effect identifiable, or report a causal number from a correlation, a regression or your own reading of a diagram. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/causal/skill))
- **g6-cegis**: Use when code has a fixed shape with a few unknown parts (constants, a sign, a comparison, a choice between strings) and you are about to pick those parts by hand from a handful of examples, or to trust generated code because it passed the examples it was written from. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/cegis/skill))
- **g6-coding-agent-gate**: Use when a coding agent is about to act on a risky plan (a deploy, auth or security, credentials, a data migration, payment, medical or legal logic, skipping or disabling a check) or is about to report coding work as done, and the plan or the drafted answer must be checked against the evidence the agent actually has. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/coding_agent_gate/skill))
- **g6-cog-arch-actr**: Use when an agent keeps many memories and needs them ranked the way human memory ranks them, by how recently and how often each was used and by the current context, with a recall refused when nothing is strong enough, or when a small rule set must consult that memory step by step and be judged by a goal test. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/cogarch/cog_arch_actr/skill))
- **g6-cog-arch-aixi**: Use when trying a model-based planning agent design on a small environment and it must be judged by a test rather than by argument, so an agent acting on a model that has not learned the environment's rewards is caught by its regret against a random policy on the same seed. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/cogarch/cog_arch_aixi/skill))
- **g6-cog-arch-gps**: Use when an agent needs a short sequence of steps to reach a goal from declared actions (each with preconditions, what it adds and what it removes), or must check a plan someone else wrote, a model's included, step by step before acting on it, so a plan that skips a precondition or undoes an earlier goal is caught before anyone follows it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/cogarch/cog_arch_gps/skill))
- **g6-cognitive-bridge**: Use when an agent must choose which way to check a research or engineering claim (a rule graph, a causal model, a formal check, a predictive model or an optimisation), rank discriminating probes between competing hypotheses, or assemble the evidence for a claim against a pinned standard, with the choice and the evidence kept apart from the agent that made the claim. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/cognitive_bridge/skill))
- **g6-complexity**: Use when a coding agent is about to change a Python package and you need to know, before it runs anything, which modules, shared state and side effects the change can reach, which of your stated requirements it must keep, and which pieces of state you marked avoidable could be simplified. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/complexity/skill))
- **g6-component-creator**: Use when an agent is starting a new G6 component, wrapping a Python library as one, or about to call a component package done, and needs the library's own shape scaffolded (declarations, a typed input, refusing handlers, a failing test, a skill whose ops match) or a package checked against the library's standard first. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/code/component_creator/skill))
- **g6-context**: Use when repository files, retrieved passages, notes, subagent output or a long conversation are about to go into a model call and could overflow the budget, bury the task, carry secrets, or carry instructions the model should only read; or when an error report from a traceback must not leak code or personal data. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/context/skill))
- **g6-csf**: Use when an agent's plan could reach a harmful state and nobody has worked out how likely that is: write the agent as a small model of states and transition probabilities with the unsafe states named, then get an upper bound on reaching one within N steps, check a temporal property on a trace or on every path, check whether the plan's operations can be undone, or checkpoint a git working tree before risky edits. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/csf/skill))
- **g6-ctx-ace**: Use when an agent must keep notes or lessons across steps or sessions and find the relevant ones again later, so an old but relevant note is not buried under newer ones, a stale note is not used as current, a note planted from injected text comes back flagged with its writer and source marked as claims, and a lesson nobody approved is held back. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/memory/ctx_ace/skill))
- **g6-ctx-cognee**: Use when facts an agent needs are spread across several texts with nothing linking them, and the person runs a Cognee server, to add the texts to it, search it, and follow the chain of links Cognee's graph draws from a fact in one text to a related fact in another, each result untrusted and cited as far as Cognee allows. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/memory/ctx_cognee/skill))
- **g6-ctx-colbert**: Use when an agent must rank a set of passages it holds by meaning rather than shared words (a question worded differently from the text that answers it), and must know which method ranked them, so word matching never stands in silently for meaning, model weights are never downloaded behind its back, and nothing from one call's passages is kept for the next. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/knowledge/ctx_colbert/skill))
- **g6-ctx-contexthub**: Use when an agent should answer from a versioned documentation registry the person trusts (context-hub's registry.json format), with each document's date, version and time to live kept, so a copy past its time to live is refused rather than used as current, and notes stay apart from the documents. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/knowledge/ctx_contexthub/skill))
- **g6-ctx-elastic**: Use when an agent must search, read or count documents in an Elasticsearch server the person runs, over fields the index's mapping really holds, so a query that cannot match is refused instead of answered with no hits, a partial answer says so, and every hit comes back as untrusted text with its address on the server. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/knowledge/ctx_elastic/skill))
- **g6-ctx-geo**: Use when an agent must answer a location question (an address, a point, a route, a distance, a height, a time zone) from a map service rather than from memory, and must know when the service refused the key or ran out of quota rather than found nothing; paid calls go out only on the action gate's allow and the person's key file. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/sources/ctx_geo/skill))
- **g6-ctx-git**: Use when an agent must read or change a git repository and every call must leave a record, or must find out what changed in a working copy since a point, whoever changed it, so changes an agent made behind the record are not missed; git runs with the repository's own programs switched off, only in folders the person declared. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/sources/ctx_git/skill))
- **g6-ctx-langextract**: Use when an agent must pull named fields (a date, a reference, an amount, a name) out of free text and needs each value tied to where it sits in the text, so a value the model invented or reworded is refused or flagged rather than passed as found, and a long text is read whole, never cut off (a field the model leaves empty is reported not checked: a fact it passes over is not caught). ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/sources/ctx_langextract/skill))
- **g6-ctx-library-mapper**: Use when an agent is about to write code for a method that may already exist as a library, or is choosing a dependency, and must find candidate libraries in PyPI, GitHub and Hugging Face with the licence each declares, and look those licences up against the project's own; a source that could not be asked is never read as "no library exists". ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/sources/ctx_library_mapper/skill))
- **g6-ctx-search**: Use when an agent must search the web and know exactly what came back, so an engine that failed, was blocked or ran out of quota is never read as "there are no results", no other engine silently answers in its place, and a paid search engine is used only with the person's own key. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/knowledge/ctx_search/skill))
- **g6-ctx-vision**: Use when an agent must see what is in a picture (describe it, answer a question about it, read its text, list its objects) through an image-capable model it was given, and must keep the model's account as a claim, with the picture checked before it is sent and the text reading cross-checked by Tesseract when that matters. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/sources/ctx_vision/skill))
- **g6-database**: Use when you need to read or change data in a database the person running G6 has declared (SQLite by default, DuckDB for large datasets or files, PostgreSQL), and want reads that cannot write, changes dry-run first in one transaction, destructive statements refused unless the server allows them, and bounded results that say when they were cut. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/integrations/database/skill))
- **g6-datalog**: Use when an answer depends on what follows from a set of facts and rules (permissions, eligibility, dependencies, reachability, policy, family or ownership relations) and you are about to say that something does or does not follow, or to list everything that does, from your own reading of the rules. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/datalog/skill))
- **g6-debate**: Use when an agent is about to accept one model's first answer to a judgement question (a plan, a design trade-off, a go or no-go) and wants role-named critics to push on it in turn, with dissent surfaced and an honest reading of how far the turns agree, which is never a check that an answer is right. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/debate/skill))
- **g6-decision-making**: Use when an agent must choose what to try next (which experiment, which arm, which action in a small known model) or how many times to retry each task within a budget, and the choice must be a standard rule with its working shown and the same answer on every run, rather than an ad hoc guess. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/cogarch/decision_making/skill))
- **g6-delivery-status-judging**: 발송 기록 1건과 그 건에 대한 메일 시스템 응답(바운스 코드·회신 도착 여부)을 받아 [대기중]/[회신]/[무응답]/[주소 오류] 넷 중 하나로 상태를 판정하고, G7 정정 신호가 오면 그 판정을 되돌린다. 발송 결과 판정(G6) 설계서와 판정 기준 카드를 그대로 실행하는 호출 계약이다. 같은 발송 건을 시간을 두고 다시 조회하는 반복 실행이다. 사용자가 "바운스 왔어", "발송 결과 확인해줘", "G6 돌려줘", "자동응답이었대 정정해줘"라고 할 때도 이 스킬이다. ([lgosoup/keunsamchon-agent-plan](https://github.com/lgosoup/keunsamchon-agent-plan/tree/HEAD/Harness/skills/g6-delivery-status-judging))
- **g6-design-governance**: Use when an agent has changed a codebase's structure (moved code, added imports, split a module) and must check before reporting it done that it added no import cycle, broke no layer rule and added no wrapper that only forwards its arguments; it reads a file map you give for any package and refuses rather than calling a check with nothing to check clean. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/code/design_governance/skill))
- **g6-distillation**: Use when a small generated or distilled Python function (a solve(x) meant to replace a model call or a hand-written rule) is about to be relied on, and you need to know whether it holds on cases it was not made from, run out of process under a time and memory bound. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/distillation/skill))
- **g6-duty-of-care**: Use when an agent works with people who may be in distress (a support chat, a care or clinical intake, a wellbeing check, a carer's notes) and must not miss signs that a vulnerable person is at risk of suicide, self-harm, harming someone else or a child being in danger; or when a PHQ-9 or GAD-7 needs adding up exactly as its form prints it; or when a dated crisis line for a region is needed. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/jobs/duty_of_care/skill))
- **g6-epistemic-integrity**: Use when a conclusion is about to be stated or acted on and its confidence must match its evidence, such as a recommendation someone will act on, a health, legal, security or financial claim, an irreversible step, a percentage without its baseline, causal language, or several sources that may all repeat one origin. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/epistemic_integrity/skill))
- **g6-evidence**: Use when a piece of work (a patch, a report, any artifact) must be given a completion state that says only what its checks support, with every gap named, or when a certificate someone hands you must be checked for consistency with the artifact's bytes. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/evidence/skill))
- **g6-formal**: Use when an answer rests on a logical step that must be exactly right, such as whether premises entail a conclusion, whether constraints can all hold, a counterexample to a claim, two conditions being equivalent, which requirements conflict, or a small optimum over integer or real constraints, and you are about to argue it in words instead of deciding it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/formal/skill))
- **g6-gate-execution**: G6发布阶段门禁执行器，统一运行构建制品校验和回滚手册完整性检查并汇总结果 ([zhuyuloveai/teams-talent-demo](https://github.com/zhuyuloveai/teams-talent-demo/tree/HEAD/workspace/.agents/skills/g6-gate-execution))
- **g6-goal-engine**: Use when an agent loop runs a long, multi-step goal and must check before each step that it is still inside its declared token, time and disk limits, that the action it is about to take is allowed, and that a person has signed off at the points named in advance; it reads declared controls against what the executor reports and executes nothing itself. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/goal_engine/skill))
- **g6-graph-reasoning**: Use when conclusions spread over a graph of people, services, accounts or documents from facts that are only partly certain (trust, risk, popularity, reliability scores) and you are about to state how sure a conclusion is, or to combine rules that might contradict each other, from your own reading. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/graph_reasoning/skill))
- **g6-grounding**: Use when a draft (an AI answer, a report, an email, a summary) states facts, figures or quotes that should come from documents you hold, and each sentence must be matched to a source sentence, with wrong numbers and flipped negations flagged, or must rest on more than one independent source, before it goes out. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/grounding/skill))
- **g6-guards**: Use when untrusted text (user input, a retrieved page, a tool result, a planner's output, an action's arguments) is about to reach an agent or a policy check and needs a cheap advisory flag for prompt injection, a request for the system prompt or an instruction to switch off a safeguard, or when code is reviewed for lines that quietly defeat a safety chain. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/guards/skill))
- **g6-harness**: Use when a task or sequence of steps needs frozen acceptance checks, observed failures, readiness measurements, or a report of which failed criterion to address next. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/harness/skill))
- **g6-hat-orchestrator**: Use when coordinating declared team capabilities, task reports and readiness barriers, while keeping authority in an operator-owned roster and escalating ties or possible real-world actions to a person through G6 action gating. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/integrations/hat_orchestrator/skill))
- **g6-intent-engine**: Use when an agent is about to start on a request whose purpose, standard of success, unacceptable outcomes, scope or risk tolerance it has not been told, and should ask the person instead of reading the request its own way; or when it needs the intent contract a mission or intake is held to. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/intent_engine/skill))
- **g6-invariants**: Use when a result envelope (from a G6 component, your own tool or another agent) is about to be trusted, passed on or asserted in a test, and you need to know whether its completion_state, warning_card, evidence, request_id and run_id agree with one another, or whether a set of checks passed only because none of them ran. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/invariants/skill))
- **g6-knowledge-chunker**: Use when a document is to be split into passages for indexing or citation and every passage must be traceable to the exact words of its source, or when a citation an agent holds (offsets and text) must be checked against the document it names before it is quoted. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/knowledge/knowledge_chunker/skill))
- **g6-knowledge-converter**: Use when a PDF, a web page, Markdown or a text file must be read as text an agent can cite, and it matters what was not read, so a page without a text layer, hidden page text or a format nothing converts is reported rather than silently dropped or read as empty. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/knowledge/knowledge_converter/skill))
- **g6-knowledge-fetcher**: Use when an agent must read a web page or search arXiv, an OAI-PMH repository, OpenAlex or Semantic Scholar, and must know exactly what came back, so a blocked, failed or cut-short fetch is never read as "there is nothing", and fetched text is kept as untrusted data rather than instructions. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/knowledge/knowledge_fetcher/skill))
- **g6-knowledge-indexer**: Use when an agent must keep many cited passages searchable between calls within a storage budget it sets, and find them again with each hit's source and a score whose scale is stated, so an index never outgrows its budget and an index that holds nothing or failed is never read as "no match". ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/knowledge/knowledge_indexer/skill))
- **g6-knowledge-manifest**: Use when the passages an agent cites must be kept with their sources, and before an answer that quotes one is relied on, to check the passage against its source as read again, so a citation to text that has since changed, moved or gone is caught rather than repeated. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/knowledge/knowledge_manifest/skill))
- **g6-lean**: Use when you have written or been handed a Lean 4 proof and are about to say it proves a theorem, or when a claim needs a machine-checked proof that a person will inspect, and you need to know the theorem is really declared, has no sorry, uses only Lean's standard axioms and states what you expect; or when you have a statement in Lean and want G6 to search for a proof it then checks the same way. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/lean/skill))
- **g6-learning**: Use when an agent or prompt keeps failing the same way on tasks you can score, and you want to learn short prompt hints from those failures, keep only the ones that win on held-out paired runs, and switch each one on only when a person accepts its recommendation. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/learning/skill))
- **g6-llm-router**: Use when you are about to pick a language model for a kind of task (code generation, summarisation, review, extraction and others), want a local-first choice with an ordered fallback chain drawn only from providers you allow, want to record how a model did so later routes lean away from one that keeps failing, or need a cost estimate that says which dated price it rests on and whether that price is out of date. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/llm_router/skill))
- **g6-meta-programming**: Use when an agent is about to rename a Python name or move statements into a new function and must change exactly the identifiers and statements meant, keeping every comment, instead of a text edit that also hits strings, attributes, keyword arguments and other scopes' names; or needs a module's functions, classes and imports read from its syntax tree. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/code/meta_programming/skill))
- **g6-metaharness**: Use when a repeated task fails, costs too much or runs slowly, and you have labelled development and held-out cases for testing proposed changes before a person activates them. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/metaharness/skill))
- **g6-model-checking**: Use when a design has states and steps (a protocol, a lock, a retry loop, a workflow, a state machine, concurrent or distributed steps) and you are about to say it never reaches a bad state, never deadlocks or can always reach a goal, from tests or a reading that tried only some orders of events. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/model_checking/skill))
- **g6-navigator**: Use when an agent has composed a plan of several G6 op calls and is about to run it, and wants every step checked first (the component exists and loads, the op exists and is not sensitive, the args fit the op's contract, the authority rule admits it) so the plan does not fail halfway; or when it needs one op's card from its parameter contract. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/navigator/skill))
- **g6-operator**: Use when proposed tasks need ranking by explicitly based estimates, optional model decomposition into a review queue, or one budgeted task tick whose outward drafts await a person's decision. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/operator/skill))
- **g6-optimisation**: Use when you are about to propose a schedule, allocation, assignment, route, flow, packing or production plan that must keep stated rules, call a plan the best, cheapest or fastest, or tune a non-linear formula's inputs by trial and error, and the numbers come from your own working rather than a solver. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/optimisation/skill))
- **g6-orientation**: Orientation map for the Reiser Lab G6 (and G4) modular LED arena project — which repo holds what (web tools, MATLAB/Python, firmware, hardware, course data), the cross-repo conventions (arena geometry, panel numbering + rotation direction, pattern naming + colocation, protocol↔pattern link, versioning, pixi dev env), how the web tools connect to the arena hardware (Web Serial + wire protocol + FicTrac bridge), and pointers to the authoritative deep docs. Use when getting oriented, deciding which repo a change belongs in, locating firmware/hardware/MATLAB code, onboarding a colleague, or answe… ([reiserlab/webDisplayTools](https://github.com/reiserlab/webDisplayTools/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/g6-orientation) · 2 copies)
- **g6-pattern-maker**: Create, transform, preview, inspect, compare, and validate reproducible Generation 6 LED-arena pattern files (.pat) from scientific stimulus descriptions or declarative JSON specifications. Use for G6 full-grid arena patterns, including 2x10, 3x10, and 4x10 geometries; moving bars and gratings; looming discs, annuli, and dot controls; compound objects and textures; exact phase-shifted partners; pattern provenance; or regression checks against existing patterns. ([reiserlab/webDisplayTools](https://github.com/reiserlab/webDisplayTools/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/g6-pattern-maker) · 2 copies)
- **g6-prolog**: Use when an answer depends on what a Prolog program derives (rules with recursion, lists, arithmetic or terms with structure) and you are about to state the answers, or that a goal does or does not follow, from your own reading of the program instead of running it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/prolog/skill))
- **g6-reasoning**: Use when an agent runs a reasoning method that makes several model calls (sampling, voting, self-consistency, a prompt chain, a debate) and needs hard ceilings on calls, output and time, a vote that does not mistake a plurality for a consensus, or an honest test of whether the method beats one direct call at the same compute. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/reasoning/skill))
- **g6-release**: Ship webDisplayTools changes through the two release tiers — Production (the root GitHub Pages URLs the rigs run) and Next (/next/, one frozen release candidate). Use whenever work is ready to share or merge — opening a PR for web-tool code, "push this", "merge this", "ship it", "deploy", "put it on Next / the testing build", "build a candidate", "release", "promote to production", "hotfix", "roll back", or "is it safe to release" — and to check what's live on Next. Drives scripts/tiers/candidate.py (pixi run candidate / release / tiers). Not for firmware or MATLAB repos. ([reiserlab/webDisplayTools](https://github.com/reiserlab/webDisplayTools/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/g6-release) · 2 copies)
- **g6-reliability-pipeline**: Use when non-trivial work is about to start, to check that the task has measurable acceptance criteria, to hold a risky or destructive task until the request says its risk is accepted, to run the premortem checklist of how the work can fail, and to critique a plan for missing tests or rollback. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/reliability_pipeline/skill))
- **g6-safety-engine**: Use when an agent loop or a tool-calling door needs trusted per-run limits (steps, tool calls, shell commands, files, lines, retries, wall time, privilege, cost) checked before each action, with a kill switch the planner cannot reset, or when a change touches tests, conftest.py or test configuration and you must know whether it weakened them before trusting a green run. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/safety_engine/skill))
- **g6-self-maintenance**: Use when you have measured how a multi-stage workflow performs (each stage's attempts, successes and daily capacity) and want to know which stage limits it most and which maintenance ticket to work on next within a token budget, as a plan to review that runs, reserves and approves nothing. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/self_maintenance/skill))
- **g6-self-training**: Use when a proposed agent or harness change needs risk classification, promotion rules, regression comparison, or rollback after a measured regression. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/self_training/skill))
- **g6-shared-workspace**: Use when several agents (or several runs of one) edit files in the same workspace folder and must not overwrite each other's work, with writes that say which version they read, the first write winning, a stale one refused with both hashes, locks opened only by a token, and a log of every conflict. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/workspace/shared_workspace/skill))
- **g6-solver**: Use when an agent has a set of step functions (prompts, a code runner, an evaluator) and wants them run through a fixed 15-step table in a known order, with retries and fallbacks, every missing, skipped or failed step recorded, and the final answer checked against stated properties before anyone calls the run a success. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/solver/skill))
- **g6-style**: The "G6" homepage style: champagne / metallic gold "light LED" on warm ivory (light mode) and warm black (dark mode), after the lit atrium of a Ginza department store, with its motion system: CSS-first entrances, a triangle lattice of light behind the page, LED strips, a 3D phone stack hero with real screens, animated "how it works" tiles (QR scan, NFC tap, wallet pass, URL typing) whose lines of light meet at one card, a click-to-play product tour, a black-and-gold plan, a products row, a photo closing CTA. Guidance only, no source files: exact token values (light + dark), keyframes, section… ([thangchiba/sisyo](https://github.com/thangchiba/sisyo/tree/HEAD/templates/.claude/skills/g6-style))
- **g6-swe-diagnostics**: Use when a coding agent is about to change a repository it does not know, and needs to learn first what is there, how it builds and runs its tests, what a test or source file holds, and, after a change, which files and tests it touches and whether the changed Python still parses. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/code/swe_diagnostics/skill))
- **g6-workflow**: Use when a person asks what G6 recorded about its own review work in their repository, wants to look at, correct or delete that record, or asks what is holding a task up. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/harness/workflow/skill))
- **g6-workforce**: Use when a coding agent is about to fix a failing test or change a function in a local git repository and what counts as done must be frozen and confirmed by a person before the patch is written, so the patch is checked against that standard rather than against tests the agent can change. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/workforce/skill))
- **g6-workspace-manager**: Use when an agent's task needs a folder of its own to keep files in, and every read and write must stay inside it, with '..', symbolic links, junctions, hard links and network paths refused as part of the act, and backups, restores and clean-ups that change nothing until asked. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/src/g6/workspace/workspace_manager/skill))
- **g6-wrong-name**: Use when checking that the conformance kit fails a bad extension; never install it. ([basicisabc1994/g6solver](https://github.com/basicisabc1994/g6solver/tree/HEAD/tests/conformance/fixtures/bad_extension/skill))
- **g6-발송결과판정**: 발송 기록 1건과 그 건에 대한 메일 시스템 응답(바운스 코드·회신 도착 여부)을 받아 [대기중]/[회신]/[무응답]/[주소 오류] 넷 중 하나로 상태를 판정하고, G7 정정 신호가 오면 그 판정을 되돌린다. 발송 결과 판정(G6) 설계서와 판정 기준 카드를 그대로 실행하는 호출 계약이다. 같은 발송 건을 시간을 두고 다시 조회하는 반복 실행이다. 사용자가 "바운스 왔어", "발송 결과 확인해줘", "G6 돌려줘", "자동응답이었대 정정해줘"라고 할 때도 이 스킬이다. - **g6e-ssm-access**: Use to reach the g6e (L40S) render host WITHOUT an SSH key or an open inbound port, via AWS SSM Session Manager — an interactive shell, a read-only GPU probe, and a loopback port-forward for the Omniverse viewer. - **g7-gate-execution**: G7变更阶段门禁执行器，统一运行RFC校验/风险分级/窗口排期校验并汇总结果 ([zhuyuloveai/teams-talent-demo](https://github.com/zhuyuloveai/teams-talent-demo/tree/HEAD/workspace/.agents/skills/g7-gate-execution))
- **g7-open-platform**: 通过 G7 易流开发者平台查询车辆、司机、位置轨迹、IoT 状态、安全事件、场站围栏等业务数据的用法说明 ([infometa/workbuddyskills](https://github.com/infometa/workbuddyskills/tree/HEAD/connectors/open-g7-com-cn/skills/g7-open) · ★455)
- **g7-reply-processing**: 일본어 회신 메일 1통을 해석 단위(T)와 번역 검증 단위(V) 두 서브에이전트로 나눠 돌려, 원문·한국어 해석·회신 성격 8분류·번역 검증이 붙은 레코드 1건을 만들고 수신 거부면 발송금지 목록에 1행을 올린다. 회신 해석·정리(G7) 설계서와 판정 기준 카드를 그대로 실행하는 호출 계약이다. 사용자가 "일본어 회신 왔어", "회신 해석해줘", "G7 돌려줘"라고 할 때도 이 스킬이다.
